##### **O.1 Publications and Presentations with ISS Results** 

###### **Refereed Journal Articles** 

3. P. Dehghani, J.G. Quintiere, _Theoretical Analysis and Predictions of Burning in Microgravity Using a Burning Emulator_ , Combustion and Flame, 233 (2021) 111572. <u>https://doi.org/10.1016/j.combustflame.2021.111572</u> 

2. P. Dehghani, P.B. Sunderland, J.G. Quintiere, J.L. deRis, _Burning in Microgravity: Experimental Results and Analysis_ , Combustion and Flame, 228 (2021) 315-330. <u>https://doi.org/10.1016/j.combustflame.2021.01.035</u>

##### **O.3 Awards** 

<mark>University of Maryland graduate student Michael Bustamante was selected as a NASA Space Technology Research Fellow for 2011/2012 for work on the BRE project, a distinct honor described at</mark> <u>https://fpe.umd.edu/release/fire-protection-experts-available-to-the-media.</u> 

For his work on BRE, Akshit Markan was awarded the University of Maryland Graduate School’s Outstanding Graduate Assistant Award for 2017.  Markan’s recognition for his outstanding work is further described at <u>https://fpe.umd.edu/news/story/fpe-phd-candidate-wins-2017-outstandinggraduate-assistant-award.  It explains, for example, that only the top 2% of Graduate Assistants were</u> selected for this honor. 

In 2021, BRE Co-Investigator Peter Sunderland was inducted as a Fellow in the Combustion Institute for his “ <mark>significant contributions to understanding laminar flame dynamics and soot formation and oxidation.”</mark> 

##### **O.4 Outreach** 

NASA Project Scientists Dennis Stocker created the Space Flames Facebook page (www.facebook.com/space.flames) and Flickr site <u>(https://www.flickr.com/photos/space-flames),</u> where some entries are specific to BRE.  He continues to administer these sites, where most content has been about the ACME project of which BRE is a part.  Meanwhile, Stocker has made educational and general information is available via <u>https://tinyurl.com/space-flames.  As an educational example,</u> a simple board game related to the ACME’s spherical flames experiments, which can be downloaded. As another example, an ACME information sheet providing an overview and recent status can also be downloaded through that site.  Such flyers have been distributed at meetings of The Combustion Institute and the American Society for Gravitational and Space Research (ASGSR).  Other documents, including the BRE Science Requirements Document (SRD), are available for download from <u>https://www1.grc.nasa.gov/space/iss-research/iss-fcf/cir/acme/downloads/.</u> 

The University of Maryland promoted awareness of the BRE investigation online, where that has included precursor ground-based research as well as the ISS testing, as can be seen via these links: 

- <u>Bustamante Wins Prestigious NASA Fellowship</u> 

- <u>FPEs Bustamone Goes Zero-G</u> 

- <u>FPE Ph.D. Candidate Wins 2017 Outstanding Graduate Assistant Award</u> 

- <u>UMD Experiments Launched into Orbit on SpaceX Capsule</u> 

- <u>Fires in Microgravity</u> 

- <u>UMD Fire Researchers Ignite the First of Two Space Station Experiments</u> 

- <u>A Brilliant, Yet Humble Mind: FPE Research Professor Takes His Leave</u> 

- <u>FPE's BRE Experiment on the ISS Continues</u> 

- <u>Unlocking the Mysteries of Fire ... in Space</u> 

- <u>Fire Protection Experts Available to the Media</u> 

- <u>Sunderland Gives Invited Talk at ASGSR 2021 Symposium</u>

ACME/BRE Final Report (2022) 

1 Feb. 2022 

Meanwhile, NASA promoted awareness of ISS combustion research with the video ‘The Power of <u>Studying Combustion on the ISS’ which was posted on March 13, 2018.</u> 

Awareness of ISS combustion research, including the ACME project, was promoted on Oct. 15, 2018 by The Combustion Institute in their online news article, “International Space Station (ISS) Combustion <u>Experiments.”</u> 

Stocker is involved in a broad range of NASA educational outreach to students and teachers, Scouts of both genders, and the general public.   His primary outreach focus is the fundamentals of microgravity (for which resources are available via  http://tinyurl.com/SEECmicrogravity) and human spaceflight.  But he periodically addresses other topics, e.g., where he sometimes speaks on ‘NASA and the Science of Superman’ – for example at the Superman Celebration in Metropolis, IL.  In this regard, his NASA outreach is carried out locally, regionally, nationally, and online.  For example, he regularly presents on microgravity at Space Center Houston’s Space Exploration Educators Conference (SEEC).   He has exhibited the last five National Scout Jamborees, four national Boy Scout conferences, the last three national Girl Scout conventions, and the 2019 World Scout Jamboree (which was held in the U.S.).  Stocker has been a key lead for the agency in NASA’s participation in these Scouting events, where as an example the latter outreach is visually described at <u>www.nasa.gov/feature/stem/world-scout-jamboree.html.</u>
